     1	# Progressive Web App (PWA)
     2	
     3	HAPI's web interface is a fully-featured PWA that can be installed on your phone for a native app-like experience.
     4	
     5	## What is PWA?
     6	
     7	A Progressive Web App (PWA) is a web application that can be installed on your device and works like a native app:
     8	
     9	- **Home screen icon** - Launch HAPI like any other app
    10	- **Full screen mode** - No browser chrome, immersive experience
    11	- **Offline support** - Basic functionality works without internet
    12	- **Auto-updates** - Always get the latest version
    13	
    14	## Installing HAPI PWA
    15	
    16	### Android (Chrome/Edge)
    17	
    18	1. Open HAPI in Chrome or Edge browser
    19	2. Look for the **"Install HAPI"** banner at the bottom
    20	3. Tap **"Install"**
    21	4. HAPI appears on your home screen
    22	
    23	::: tip
    24	If you don't see the install banner, tap the three-dot menu and select **"Add to Home screen"** or **"Install app"**.
    25	:::
    26	
    27	### iOS (Safari)
    28	
    29	1. Open HAPI in Safari browser
    30	2. Tap the **Share** button (square with arrow)
    31	3. Scroll down and tap **"Add to Home Screen"**
    32	4. Tap **"Add"** in the top right corner
    33	
    34	::: warning
    35	iOS requires Safari for PWA installation. Chrome/Firefox on iOS don't support the "Add to Home Screen" feature.
    36	:::
    37	
    38	### Desktop (Chrome/Edge)
    39	
    40	1. Open HAPI in your browser
    41	2. Click the install icon in the address bar (⊕)
    42	3. Or use the menu: **"Install HAPI..."**
    43	4. HAPI opens as a standalone window
    44	
    45	## PWA Features
    46	
    47	### Offline Mode
    48	
    49	When offline, HAPI can:
    50	
    51	- Display cached session lists
    52	- Show previously loaded messages
    53	- Queue actions for when you're back online
    54	
    55	An offline indicator appears when you lose connection.
    56	
    57	### Auto-Update
    58	
    59	HAPI automatically checks for updates:
    60	
    61	- Updates are checked hourly in the background
    62	- When a new version is available, you'll see a prompt
    63	- Click "Reload" to get the latest version
    64	
    65	### Background Sync
    66	
    67	Actions taken offline are synced when reconnected:
    68	
    69	- Pending messages are sent
    70	- Permission decisions are relayed
    71	- Session state is refreshed
    72	
    73	## Caching Strategy
    74	
    75	HAPI uses intelligent caching:
    76	
    77	| Content | Strategy | Duration |
    78	|---------|----------|----------|
    79	| App shell | Cache first | Until update |
    80	| Sessions API | Network first | 5 minutes |
    81	| Machines API | Network first | 10 minutes |
    82	| Static assets | Cache first | Forever |
    83	
    84	## Notifications
    85	
    86	HAPI supports push notifications to alert you when agents need attention.
    87	
    88	### Enable Notifications
    89	
    90	1. Open HAPI - a permission popup appears automatically
    91	2. Tap **Allow** to enable notifications
    92	3. If you missed the popup, go to system settings to grant permission
    93	
    94	### Notification Types
    95	
    96	| Type | When Sent |
    97	|------|-----------|
    98	| Permission Request | Agent needs your approval |
    99	| Ready | Agent finished and awaits input |
   100	
   101	::: tip
   102	If push notifications don't work in your region (e.g., FCM unavailable), use [Telegram integration](./installation.md#telegram-setup) instead.
   103	:::
   104	
   105	## Managing Your PWA
   106	
   107	### Check Install Status
   108	
   109	HAPI shows different UI based on install status:
   110	
   111	- **Not installed** - Shows install prompt
   112	- **Installing** - Shows progress indicator
   113	- **Installed** - No prompt shown
   114	
   115	### Uninstalling
   116	
   117	**Android:**
   118	1. Long-press the HAPI icon
   119	2. Drag to "Uninstall" or tap the X
   120	
   121	**iOS:**
   122	1. Long-press the HAPI icon
   123	2. Tap "Remove App" → "Delete App"
   124	
   125	**Desktop:**
   126	1. Open HAPI
   127	2. Click the three-dot menu
   128	3. Select "Uninstall HAPI"
   129	
   130	### Clearing Cache
   131	
   132	If you experience issues:
   133	
   134	1. Open HAPI in browser (not installed version)
   135	2. Open Developer Tools (F12)
   136	3. Go to Application → Storage
   137	4. Click "Clear site data"
   138	
   139	## Best Practices
   140	
   141	### Battery Optimization
   142	
   143	On Android, disable battery optimization for HAPI to ensure:
   144	- Background sync works reliably
   145	- Notifications arrive promptly
   146	
   147	Settings → Apps → HAPI → Battery → Unrestricted
   148	
   149	### Data Usage
   150	
   151	HAPI uses minimal data:
   152	
   153	- Initial load: ~500KB
   154	- Cached after first load
   155	- Only syncs changed data
   156	
   157	### Multiple Devices
   158	
   159	You can install HAPI on multiple devices:
   160	
   161	- All devices use the same server
   162	- Sessions sync across devices
   163	- Same access token works everywhere
   164	
   165	## Troubleshooting
   166	
   167	### Install Button Not Showing
   168	
   169	- Ensure you're using HTTPS (required for PWA)
   170	- Try refreshing the page
   171	- Check if already installed
   172	
   173	### App Not Updating
   174	
   175	1. Close the app completely
   176	2. Reopen and wait for update prompt
   177	3. If stuck, clear cache and reinstall
   178	
   179	### Offline Mode Not Working
   180	
   181	- Ensure you've loaded the app at least once online
   182	- Check if ServiceWorker is registered (DevTools → Application)
   183	- Clear cache and reload
   184	
   185	### iOS-Specific Issues
   186	
   187	- Must use Safari for installation
   188	- No background sync on iOS
   189	- Limited offline capabilities
   190	
   191	## Telegram Mini App Alternative
   192	
   193	If PWA doesn't suit your needs, consider the Telegram Mini App:
   194	
   195	- Works inside Telegram
   196	- No separate installation
   197	- Same features as PWA
   198	- Integrated notifications
   199	
   200	See [Installation Guide](./installation.md#telegram-setup) for Telegram setup.

    26	
    27	### Do I need a hub?
    28	
    29	HAPI includes an embedded hub. Just run `hapi hub` on your machine - no external hub required.
    30	
    31	`hapi server` remains supported as an alias.
    32	
    33	### How do I access HAPI from my phone?
    34	
    35	For local network access:
    36	```
    37	http://<your-computer-ip>:3006
    38	```
    39	
    40	If your phone cannot connect, make sure the hub is not only listening on `127.0.0.1`. For LAN access, set `listenHost` to `0.0.0.0` in `~/.hapi/settings.json` or set `HAPI_LISTEN_HOST=0.0.0.0`, then restart `hapi hub`.
    41	
    42	For internet access:
    43	- If the hub has a public IP, access it directly (use HTTPS via reverse proxy for production)
    44	- If behind NAT, set up a tunnel (Cloudflare Tunnel, Tailscale, or ngrok)
    45	
    46	### What's the access token for?
    47	
    48	The `CLI_API_TOKEN` is a shared secret that authenticates:
    49	- CLI connections to the hub
    50	- Web app logins
    51	- Telegram account binding
    52	
    53	It's auto-generated on first hub start and saved to `~/.hapi/settings.json`.
    54	
    55	### Do you support multiple accounts?
    56	
    57	Yes. We support lightweight multi-account access via namespaces for shared team hubs. See [Namespace (Advanced)](./namespace.md).
    58	
    59	### Can I use HAPI without Telegram?
    60	
    61	Yes. Telegram is optional. You can use the web app directly in any browser or install it as a PWA.
    62	
    63	## Usage
    64	
    65	### How do I approve permissions remotely?
    66	
    67	1. When your AI agent requests permission (e.g., to edit a file), you'll see a notification
    68	2. Open HAPI on your phone
    69	3. Navigate to the active session
    70	4. Approve or deny the pending permission
    71	
    72	### How do I receive notifications?
    73	
    74	HAPI supports two methods:
    75	
    76	1. **PWA Push Notifications** - Enable when prompted, works even when app is closed
    77	2. **Telegram Bot** - See [Telegram Setup](./installation.md#telegram-setup)
    78	
    79	### Can I start sessions remotely?
    80	
    81	Yes, with runner mode:
    82	
    83	1. Run `hapi runner start` on your computer
    84	2. Your machine appears in the "Machines" list in the web app
    85	3. Tap to spawn new sessions from anywhere
    86	
    87	### How do I see what files were changed?
    88	
    89	In the session view, tap the "Files" tab to:
    90	- Browse project files
    91	- View git status
    92	- See diffs of changed files
    93	
    94	### Can I send messages to the AI from my phone?
    95	
    96	Yes. Open any session and use the chat interface to send messages directly to the AI agent.
    97	
    98	### Can I access a terminal remotely?
    99	
   100	Yes, on Linux and macOS hosts. Open a session in the web app and tap the Terminal tab for a remote shell.
   101	
   102	Windows hosts do not support the remote Terminal yet because the Bun PTY API used by HAPI is currently POSIX-only.
   103	
   104	### How do I use voice control?
   105	
   106	Set `ELEVENLABS_API_KEY`, open a session in the web app, and click the microphone button. See [Voice Assistant](./voice-assistant.md).
   107	
   108	## Security
   109	
   110	### Is my data safe?
   111	
   112	Yes. HAPI is local-first:
   113	- All data stays on your machine
   114	- Nothing is uploaded to external servers
   115	- The database is stored locally in `~/.hapi/`
   116	
   117	### How secure is the token authentication?
   118	
   119	The auto-generated token is 256-bit (cryptographically secure). For external access, always use HTTPS via a tunnel.
   120	
   121	### Can others access my HAPI instance?
   122	
   123	Only if they have your access token. For additional security:
   124	- Use a strong, unique token
   125	- Always use HTTPS for external access
   126	- Consider Tailscale for private networking
   127	
   128	## Troubleshooting
   129	
   130	### "Connection refused" error
   131	
   132	- Ensure hub is running: `hapi hub`
   133	- Check firewall allows port 3006
   134	- Verify `HAPI_API_URL` is correct
   135	
   136	### My phone cannot access HAPI on the local network
   137	
   138	If HAPI works on your computer but not from another device on the same LAN, check the hub bind address first. By default, HAPI listens on `127.0.0.1`, which only accepts localhost connections.
   139	
   140	Use one of these:
   141	
   142	```json
   143	{
   144	  "listenHost": "0.0.0.0"
   145	}
   146	```
   147	
   148	```bash
   149	export HAPI_LISTEN_HOST=0.0.0.0
   150	```
   151	
   152	Then restart `hapi hub` and open:
   153	
   154	```bash
   155	http://<your-computer-ip>:3006
   156	```
   157	
   158	Also verify your OS firewall allows inbound connections on port `3006`.
   159	
   160	### "Invalid token" error

```

    95	</details>
    96	
    97	<details>
    98	<summary>Prebuilt binary</summary>
    99	
   100	Download the latest release from [GitHub Releases](https://github.com/tiann/hapi/releases).
   101	
   102	```bash
   103	xattr -d com.apple.quarantine ./hapi
   104	chmod +x ./hapi
   105	sudo mv ./hapi /usr/local/bin/
   106	```
   107	</details>
   108	
   109	<details>
   110	<summary>Build from source</summary>
   111	
   112	```bash
   113	git clone https://github.com/tiann/hapi.git
   114	cd hapi
   115	bun install
   116	bun build:single-exe
   117	
   118	./cli/dist/hapi
   119	```

   121	
   122	## Hub setup
   123	
   124	The hub can be deployed on:
   125	
   126	- **Local desktop** (default) - Run on your development machine
   127	- **Remote host** - Deploy the hub on a VPS, cloud host, or any machine with network access
   128	
   129	### Default: Public Relay (recommended)
   130	
   131	```bash
   132	hapi hub --relay
   133	```
   134	
   135	The terminal displays a URL and QR code. Scan to access from anywhere.
   136	
   137	`hapi server` remains supported as an alias.
   138	
   139	- **End-to-end encrypted** with WireGuard + TLS
   140	- No configuration needed
   141	- Works behind NAT, firewalls, and any network
   142	
   143	> **Tip:** The relay uses UDP by default. If you experience connectivity issues, set `HAPI_RELAY_FORCE_TCP=true` to force TCP mode.
   144	
   145	### Local Only
   146	
   147	```bash
   148	hapi hub
   149	# or
   150	hapi hub --no-relay
   151	```
   152	
   153	The hub listens on `http://localhost:3006` by default.
   154	
   155	On first run, HAPI:
   156	
   157	1. Creates `~/.hapi/`
   158	2. Generates a secure access token
   159	3. Prints the token and saves it to `~/.hapi/settings.json`
   160	
   161	<details>
   162	<summary>Config files</summary>
   163	
   164	```
   165	~/.hapi/
   166	├── settings.json      # Main configuration
   167	├── hapi.db           # SQLite database (hub)
   168	├── runner.state.json  # Runner process state
   169	└── logs/             # Log files
   170	```
   171	</details>
   172	
   173	<details>
   174	<summary>Environment variables</summary>
   175	
   176	| Variable | Default | settings.json | Description |
   177	|----------|---------|---------------|-------------|
   178	| `CLI_API_TOKEN` | Auto-generated | `cliApiToken` | Shared secret for authentication |
   179	| `HAPI_API_URL` | `http://localhost:3006` | `apiUrl` | Hub URL for CLI connections |
   180	| `HAPI_EXTRA_HEADERS_JSON` | - | - | JSON object of extra outbound headers for CLI → hub HTTP/WebSocket requests |
   181	| `HAPI_LISTEN_HOST` | `127.0.0.1` | `listenHost` | Hub HTTP bind address |
   182	| `HAPI_LISTEN_PORT` | `3006` | `listenPort` | Hub HTTP port |
   183	| `HAPI_PUBLIC_URL` | - | `publicUrl` | Public URL for external access |
   184	| `CORS_ORIGINS` | - | `corsOrigins` | Allowed CORS origins (comma-separated) |
   185	| `TELEGRAM_BOT_TOKEN` | - | `telegramBotToken` | Telegram Bot API token |
   186	| `TELEGRAM_NOTIFICATION` | `true` | `telegramNotification` | Enable Telegram notifications |
   187	| `HAPI_RELAY_FORCE_TCP` | `false` | - | Force TCP mode for relay |
   188	| `VAPID_SUBJECT` | `mailto:admin@hapi.run` | - | Web Push contact info |
   189	| `HAPI_HOME` | `~/.hapi` | - | Config directory path |
   190	| `DB_PATH` | `~/.hapi/hapi.db` | - | Database file path |
   191	| `ELEVENLABS_API_KEY` | - | - | ElevenLabs API key for voice |
   192	| `ELEVENLABS_AGENT_ID` | Auto-created | - | Custom ElevenLabs agent ID |
   193	</details>
   194	
   195	<details>
   196	<summary>settings.json example</summary>
   197	
   198	Configuration priority: **ENV > settings.json > default**
   199	
   200	When ENV values are set and not present in settings.json, they are automatically saved.
   201	
   202	```json
   203	{
   204	  "$schema": "https://hapi.run/docs/schemas/settings.schema.json",
   205	  "listenHost": "0.0.0.0",
   206	  "listenPort": 3006,
   207	  "publicUrl": "https://your-domain.com"
   208	}
   209	```
   210	
   211	JSON Schema: [settings.schema.json](https://hapi.run/schemas/settings.schema.json)
   212	</details>
   213	
   214	## CLI setup
   215	
   216	If the hub is not on localhost, set these before running `hapi`:
   217	
   218	```bash
   219	export HAPI_API_URL="http://your-hub:3006"
   220	export CLI_API_TOKEN="your-token-here"
   221	export HAPI_EXTRA_HEADERS_JSON='{"Cookie":"CF_Authorization=..."}'
   222	```
   223	
   224	Or use interactive login:
   225	
   226	```bash
   227	hapi auth login
   228	```
   229	
   230	Authentication commands:
   231	
   232	```bash
   233	hapi auth status
   234	hapi auth login
   235	hapi auth logout
   236	```
   237	
   238	Each machine gets a unique ID stored in `~/.hapi/settings.json`. This allows:
   239	
   240	- Multiple machines to connect to one hub
   241	- Remote session spawning on specific machines
   242	- Machine health monitoring
   243	
   244	## Operations
   245	
   246	### Self-hosted tunnels
   247	
   248	If you prefer not to use the public relay (e.g., for lower latency or self-managed infrastructure), you can use these alternatives:
   249	
   250	<details>
   251	<summary>Cloudflare Tunnel</summary>
   252	
   253	https://developers.cloudflare.com/cloudflare-one/connections/connect-networks/
   254	
   255	> **Note:** Cloudflare Quick Tunnels (TryCloudflare) are not supported because they [do not support SSE](https://developers.cloudflare.com/cloudflare-one/networks/connectors/cloudflare-tunnel/do-more-with-tunnels/trycloudflare/), which HAPI uses for real-time updates. Use a Named Tunnel instead.
   256	
   257	**Named tunnel setup:**
   258	
   259	```bash
   260	# Install cloudflared: https://developers.cloudflare.com/cloudflare-one/connections/connect-networks/downloads/
   261	
   262	# Create and configure a named tunnel
   263	cloudflared tunnel create hapi
   264	cloudflared tunnel route dns hapi hapi.yourdomain.com
   265	
   266	# Run the tunnel
   267	cloudflared tunnel --protocol http2 run hapi
   268	```
   269	
   270	> **Tip:** Use `--protocol http2` instead of QUIC (the default) to avoid potential timeout issues with long-lived connections.
   271	
   272	</details>
   273	
   274	<details>
   275	<summary>Tailscale</summary>
   276	
   277	https://tailscale.com/download
   278	
   279	```bash
   280	sudo tailscale up
   281	hapi hub
   282	```
   283	
   284	Access via your Tailscale IP:
   285	
   286	```
   287	http://100.x.x.x:3006
   288	```
   289	</details>
   290	
   291	<details>
   292	<summary>Public IP / Reverse Proxy</summary>
   293	
   294	If the hub has a public IP, access directly via `http://your-hub-ip:3006`.
   295	
   296	Use HTTPS (via Nginx, Caddy, etc.) for production.
   297	
   298	**Self-signed certificates (HTTPS)**
   299	
   300	If `HAPI_API_URL` is set to an `https://...` URL with a self-signed (or otherwise untrusted) certificate, the CLI may fail with:
   301	
   302	```
   303	Error: self signed certificate
   304	```
   305	
   306	Recommended fixes (in order):
   307	
   308	1. Use a publicly trusted certificate (e.g., Let's Encrypt)
   309	2. Trust your private CA (recommended for private networks)
   310	3. Dev-only workaround: disable TLS verification (insecure)
   311	
   312	```bash
   313	# Preferred: trust your own CA
   314	export NODE_EXTRA_CA_CERTS="/path/to/your-ca.pem"
   315	
   316	# Dev-only workaround: disable TLS verification (INSECURE)
   317	export NODE_TLS_REJECT_UNAUTHORIZED=0
   318	```
   319	
   320	If you use the dev-only workaround, assume MITM risk; do not use on public networks.
   321	
   322	</details>
   323	
   324	### Telegram setup
   325	
   326	Enable Telegram notifications and Mini App access:
   327	
   328	1. Message [@BotFather](https://t.me/BotFather) and create a bot
   329	2. Set the bot token and public URL
   330	3. Start the hub and bind your account
   331	
   332	```bash
   333	export TELEGRAM_BOT_TOKEN="your-bot-token"
   334	export HAPI_PUBLIC_URL="https://your-public-url"
   335	
   336	hapi hub
   337	```
   338	
   339	Then message your bot with `/start`, open the app, and enter your `CLI_API_TOKEN`.
   340	
   341	**Troubleshooting:**
   342	
   343	- If binding fails, verify `HAPI_PUBLIC_URL` is accessible from the internet
   344	- Telegram Mini App requires HTTPS (not HTTP)
   345	
   346	### Runner setup
   347	
   348	Run a background service for remote session spawning:
   349	
   350	```bash
   351	hapi runner start
   352	hapi runner status
   353	hapi runner logs
   354	hapi runner stop
   355	```
   356	
   357	With the runner running:
   358	
   359	- Your machine appears in the "Machines" list
   360	- You can spawn sessions remotely from the web app

```

手机上的 HAPI 不是去应用商店下载的独立 App，它本质上是个可安装到手机桌面的 `PWA`。[pwa.md](/Users/lyston/PycharmProjects/hapi/docs/guide/pwa.md#L1)

最省事的做法是：

1. 在电脑上启动 HAPI：
```bash
hapi hub --relay
```
2. 终端会显示一个 URL 和二维码，用手机打开或扫码。[quick-start.md](/Users/lyston/PycharmProjects/hapi/docs/guide/quick-start.md#L27)
3. 第一次登录时，输入 `CLI_API_TOKEN`。这个 token 在 `~/.hapi/settings.json` 里，也会在第一次启动时打印出来。[installation.md](/Users/lyston/PycharmProjects/hapi/docs/guide/installation.md#L155)
4. 打开后把它“装到桌面”：
   - 安卓：用 Chrome/Edge 打开，点底部 `Install HAPI`，或者浏览器菜单里的 `Add to Home screen / Install app`。[pwa.md](/Users/lyston/PycharmProjects/hapi/docs/guide/pwa.md#L16)
   - iPhone：必须用 Safari 打开，点分享按钮，再点 `Add to Home Screen`。[pwa.md](/Users/lyston/PycharmProjects/hapi/docs/guide/pwa.md#L27)
5. 安装后允许通知，这样 agent 要你审批权限时，手机会收到提醒。[pwa.md](/Users/lyston/PycharmProjects/hapi/docs/guide/pwa.md#L84)

如果你只想局域网访问，也可以不用 relay：
```bash
export HAPI_LISTEN_HOST=0.0.0.0
hapi hub
```
然后手机打开：
```text
http://你的电脑IP:3006
```
如果手机打不开，通常是因为 HAPI 只监听了 `127.0.0.1`，或者电脑防火墙没放行 `3006` 端口。[faq.md](/Users/lyston/PycharmProjects/hapi/docs/guide/faq.md#L33)

如果你想走 Telegram 里的“迷你应用”，那是另一种入口，不用单独装桌面 App。做法是先配置：
```bash
export TELEGRAM_BOT_TOKEN="你的-bot-token"
export HAPI_PUBLIC_URL="https://你的公网地址"
hapi hub
```
然后在 Telegram 里找你的 bot，发 `/start` 打开即可。注意这个方式必须是 `HTTPS`。[installation.md](/Users/lyston/PycharmProjects/hapi/docs/guide/installation.md#L324)

如果你愿意，我可以下一步直接按你的手机类型，给你写一版“iPhone 安装 HAPI”或“安卓安装 HAPI”的一步一步操作。

> AGENT

我已经定位到二维码打印逻辑了：它不是在生成 token 后立刻显示，而是要等 relay 建好、再等 TLS 证书准备好之后才会打印。所以你现在这个现象更像是“还没走完 tunnel 初始化”，不是 token 有问题。

> AGENT

不是 token 生成完就立刻出二维码。

按代码看，`hapi hub --relay` 要先：

1. 建好 relay tunnel
2. 再等 tunnel 的 TLS 证书就绪
3. 然后才会打印公网地址、浏览器直达链接和二维码

对应逻辑在 [index.ts](/Users/lyston/PycharmProjects/hapi/hub/src/index.ts#L252) 和 [tlsGate.ts](/Users/lyston/PycharmProjects/hapi/hub/src/tunnel/tlsGate.ts#L159)。

你现在先看终端后面有没有继续出现这些行：

```text
[Web] Hub listening on :3006
[Web] Local:  http://localhost:3006
[Tunnel] Waiting for trusted TLS certificate...
[Web] Public: https://...
Open in browser:
  https://app.hapi.run/?hub=...&token=...
or scan the QR code to open:
```

只有到 `[Web] Public:` 之后，二维码才会出来。

如果等了 20 到 60 秒还没有，先试这个：

```bash
HAPI_RELAY_FORCE_TCP=true hapi hub --relay
```

文档里也提到 relay 默认走 UDP，网络环境不友好时切 TCP 更稳。

如果你只是想先用手机打开，不等 relay，也可以直接本机局域网方式：

```bash
HAPI_LISTEN_HOST=0.0.0.0 hapi hub
```

然后手机访问：

```text
http://你的电脑IP:3006
```

当前版本的代码里，只有等 relay 域名的 TLS 证书被判定为“可信”之后，才会继续打印：

- `[Web] Public: ...`
- 浏览器直达链接

逻辑在 [index.ts](/Users/lyston/PycharmProjects/hapi/hub/src/index.ts#L252) 和 [tlsGate.ts](/Users/lyston/PycharmProjects/hapi/hub/src/tunnel/tlsGate.ts#L159)。

所以结论是：

- `hub` 已经正常启动了
- relay 隧道也起来了
- 只是公网证书这一步还没就绪，所以二维码没出来

你现在可以这样处理：

1. 先再等 1 到 3 分钟。
有时证书签发或传播会慢一点。

2. 直接在电脑浏览器试一下这个地址：
```text
https://3wsutikefydws4c4.relay.hapi.run/health
```
如果浏览器还报证书不可信，那就和日志一致，确实卡在证书。

3. 重新试一次 TCP 模式：
```bash
HAPI_RELAY_FORCE_TCP=true hapi hub --relay
```

4. 如果你只是想先让手机用上，不必等 relay，可以直接走局域网：
```bash
HAPI_LISTEN_HOST=0.0.0.0 hapi hub
```
然后手机打开：
```text
http://你的电脑IP:3006
```

5. 如果官方 relay 一直卡这一步，就改成更稳的方式：
- Cloudflare Named Tunnel
- Tailscale

还有一点，你现在即使没有二维码，也可以手动打开官方 Web App：

```text
https://app.hapi.run/?hub=https%3A%2F%2F3wsutikefydws4c4.relay.hapi.run
```

打开后再手动输入 `~/.hapi/settings.json` 里的 `CLI_API_TOKEN` 即可。

> AGENT

这次我替你实际探测过了，结论比较明确：

不是你操作错了，也不是二维码逻辑坏了，而是这个 relay 子域名当前的公网 HTTPS 端点本身没正常起来。

我从这台机器验证到的结果是：

- 域名能解析，解析到 `43.130.231.139`
- `443` 端口能连上
- 但 TLS 握手失败了
- `curl` 返回的是 `SSL_ERROR_SYSCALL`
- `openssl s_client` 返回的是 `unexpected eof while reading`

这和你日志里一直停在：

```text
[Tunnel] Waiting for trusted TLS certificate...
```

完全一致。HAPI 只有在这一步通过后，才会打印：

- `[Web] Public: ...`
- 浏览器直达链接
- 二维码

对应代码在 [index.ts](/Users/lyston/PycharmProjects/hapi/hub/src/index.ts#L252) 和 [tlsGate.ts](/Users/lyston/PycharmProjects/hapi/hub/src/tunnel/tlsGate.ts#L159)。

**所以现在的判断是**
- `hub` 正常
- relay 隧道注册正常
- 但 `https://3wsutikefydws4c4.relay.hapi.run` 这个地址的 HTTPS 握手没有成功
- 因此你本地浏览器也打不开，这不是手机问题

**最实用的处理方式**
现在不要继续死磕官方 relay，先换一种能马上用的方式。

**方案 1：局域网直连，最快**
如果手机和电脑在同一个 Wi‑Fi：

```bash
HAPI_LISTEN_HOST=0.0.0.0 hapi hub
```

然后查你电脑 IP：

```bash
ipconfig getifaddr en0
```

如果 `en0` 没结果，再试：

```bash
ipconfig getifaddr en1
```

然后手机打开：

```text
http://你的电脑IP:3006
```

如果是 iPhone，用 Safari 打开后可以“添加到主屏幕”；如果是安卓，用 Chrome 打开后安装 PWA。

注意：
- macOS 防火墙要允许 3006
- 电脑和手机要在同一网段

**方案 2：Tailscale，最稳**
如果你经常要外网访问，比官方 relay 更稳：

```bash
sudo tailscale up
HAPI_LISTEN_HOST=0.0.0.0 hapi hub
```

然后手机也装 Tailscale，打开：

```text
http://你的 Tailscale IP:3006
```

**方案 3：Cloudflare Named Tunnel**
如果你想正式公网访问，我建议直接上 Cloudflare Named Tunnel，不要依赖这个 relay。

**补充**
`HAPI_PUBLIC_URL: http://localhost:3006` 这个默认值不是这次问题的根因。真正卡住的是 relay 子域名的 HTTPS 证书/握手阶段。

如果你愿意，我下一步可以直接带你走“局域网直连”这条，我会按你的 Mac 环境一步一步给你命令，直到手机能打开为止。
